Q1. Digit sum \(8\) with tens digit \(6\) identifies which two-digit number?
A. \(63\)
B. \(61\)
C. \(64\)
D. \(62\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
6+u=8\Rightarrow u=2
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 62.

\[
N=10\times 6+2=62
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 62 is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(62\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=2
\]

\[
N=62
\]

Fast result: \(62\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
Situation: digit-interchange
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: ssc

---
Q2. For the number \(1440\), find the count of positive divisors?
A. \(35\)
B. \(38\)
C. \(36\)
D. \(37\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
1440=2^{5}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(1440)=(5+1)\times (2+1)\times (1+1)=36
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(5+1)\times (2+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_prime_composite_deduction
Situation: square-divisor-count
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

---
Q3. Two buses leave a stand every \(156\) and \(72\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(889\)
B. \(936\)
C. \(983\)
D. \(1030\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
156=2^{2}\times 3\times 13
\]

\[
72=2^{3}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(156,72)=936
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 936.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(936\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(156,72)=\frac{156\times 72}{\operatorname{HCF}(156,72)}=936
\]

Fast result: \(936\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_three_number_hcf_lcm
Situation: three-number-alignment
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

---
Q4. When \(7^{51}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(51-1)\bmod 10+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
7^{51}\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(51-1)\bmod 10+1=1
\]

\[
7^{51}\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_remainder_after_power
Situation: counter-cycle
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

Q5. When \(3\) is raised to \(73\), what two digits appear at the end?
A. \(25\)
B. \(23\)
C. \(24\)
D. \(22\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{100}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 20.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 27\rightarrow 81\rightarrow 43\rightarrow 29\rightarrow 87\rightarrow 61\rightarrow 83\rightarrow 49\rightarrow 47\rightarrow 41\rightarrow 23\rightarrow 69\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 21\rightarrow 63\rightarrow 89\rightarrow 67\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(73-1)\bmod 20+1=13
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 23.

\[
3^{73}\equiv 23\pmod{100}
\]

Answer Verification
The 13th term of the cycle is 23.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(23\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(73-1)\bmod 20+1=13
\]

\[
3^{73}\equiv 23\pmod{100}
\]

Fast result: \(23\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_last_two_digits
Situation: last-two-digit-cycle
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

Q7. When \(7^{34}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(34-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
7^{34}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(34-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
2^{34}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_modular_arithmetic
Situation: nested-remainder
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: elite

Q8. What least number should be added to \(315\) so that the result is divisible by \(11\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Combine the number conditions first

Optimization is done only after the divisibility or remainder condition is converted into one usable cycle.

Step 2: Move to the nearest valid boundary

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
315=11\times 28+7
\]

\[
11-7=4
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 4.
\[
N=4
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Use the combined cycle and take only the needed boundary correction.

\[
Z=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_minimum_addition
Situation: minimum-adjustment
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

Q9. By what least number should \(72\) be multiplied to make it a perfect square?
A. \(1\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Factorize the given number

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
72=2^{3}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{3}\to 2^{4}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=2
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_perfect_square_completion
Situation: square-completion
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: punjab_state

Q11. Prime factors of \(24\) and \(30\) are used to form their common cycle. What is the least common-cycle number greater than \(721\)?
A. \(924\)
B. \(798\)
C. \(840\)
D. \(882\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Translate every clue into a number condition

Reconstruction questions should not be guessed; each clue must become a checkable relation.

\[
m=\operatorname{LCM}(24,30)
\]

Step 2: Carry the result through the next condition

This makes the final candidate satisfy the full chain, not only the first clue.

\[
N=840
\]

Answer Verification
The reconstructed value is 840.
\[
N=840
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(840\).

Shortcut

Use the strongest clue first, then verify the candidate against the remaining condition.

\[
Z=840
\]

Fast result: \(840\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(86-1)\bmod 10+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
13^{86}\equiv 9\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(86-1)\bmod 10+1=6
\]

\[
2^{86}\equiv 9\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q17. Number \(45\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 4+5=45
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 5+4=54
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |4-5|=9\times 1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |4-5|=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_digit_interchange
Situation: largest-digit-number
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

Q18. In \(25!\), find the exponent of \(3\) in its prime factorisation?
A. \(9\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 3 in n!

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{3}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
8+2=10
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 3 dividing 25! is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{25}{3}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{25}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_factorial_divisibility
Situation: trailing-zero-count
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: ssc

Q26. When \(3^{27}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(27-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{27}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(27-1)\bmod 3+1=3
\]

\[
3^{27}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q27. For exponent \(67\), what ending digit does \(8^{67}\) have?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
8\equiv 8\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(67-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
8^{67}\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(67-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
8^{67}\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Family: ns_unit_digit_cycle
Situation: reverse-ending
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

Q28. Tens digit \(7\) and ones digit \(2\) are used in a number. How much does it change when reversed?
A. \(44\)
B. \(47\)
C. \(45\)
D. \(46\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+2=72
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 2+7=27
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-2|=9\times 5=45
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 45.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(45\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-2|=45
\]

Fast result: \(45\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q35. Bells ring at intervals of \(36\) and \(153\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(673\)
B. \(581\)

D. \(643\)
Answer: C

Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[

\]

\[
153=3^{2}\times 17
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(36,153)=612
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 612.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(612\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(36,153)=\frac{36\times 153}{\operatorname{HCF}(36,153)}=612
\]

Fast result: \(612\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q46. Between \(138\) and \(252\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(16\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 138 is 144.

\[
8\times 18=144
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 248 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 252.

\[
252=8\times 31+4
\]

\[
252-4=248
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{248-144}{8}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{252}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{137}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
31-17=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q59. When \(11^{31}\) is divided by \(9\), what remainder is left?
A. \(1\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(31-1)\bmod 6+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
11^{31}\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(31-1)\bmod 6+1=1
\]

\[
2^{31}\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q62. Between \(207\) and \(316\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(18\)
B. \(19\)
C. \(17\)
D. \(20\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 207 is 210.

\[
6\times 35=210
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 312 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 316.

\[
316=6\times 52+4
\]

\[
316-4=312
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{312-210}{6}+1=17+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 18.
Hence, there are 18 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{316}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{206}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
52-34=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q67. The number \(4x349\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(2\)
B. \(0\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=12
\]

\[
12-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
12-x=11
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
12-1=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(36)=(2+1)\times (2+1)=9
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(2+1)\times (2+1)
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q80. When \(3^{96}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(96-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{96}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(96-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
3^{96}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Situation: power-remainder-cycle
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

Q88. Between \(213\) and \(349\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(14\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 213 is 216.

\[
12\times 18=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 348 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 349.

\[
349=12\times 29+1
\]

\[
349-1=348
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{348-216}{12}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{349}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{212}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
29-17=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Situation: range-multiple-count
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: ssc

Q89. For the number \(12\), find the product of its positive divisors?

B. \(1555\)
C. \(1728\)
D. \(1901\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 12 d=12^{d(12)/2}=12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 1728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1728\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Fast result: \(1728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\operatorname{LCM}(99,90)=990
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 990.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(990\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(99,90)=\frac{99\times 90}{\operatorname{HCF}(99,90)}=990
\]

Fast result: \(990\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q109. Between \(216\) and \(347\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: A

Explanation:

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 216 is 216.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 342 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 347.

\[
347=9\times 38+5
\]

\[
347-5=342
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{342-216}{9}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{347}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{215}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
38-23=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q111. When \(7^{53}\) is divided by \(13\), what remainder is left?
A. \(10\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
7\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(53-1)\bmod 12+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 11.

\[
7^{53}\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 11.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(53-1)\bmod 12+1=5
\]

\[
7^{53}\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Situation: power-remainder-cycle
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[

\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(95-1)\bmod 10+1=5
\]

\[
7^{95}\equiv 10\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 31.

\[
N=10\times 3+1=31
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 31 is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(31\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=1
\]

\[
N=31
\]

Fast result: \(31\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(78-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[

\]

Final Answer

Shortcut

\[
p=(78-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

\[
6^{78}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Answer: C

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=19
\]

\[
S+x=19+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q207. The expression \(11^{88}\) is reduced modulo \(9\). What remainder is obtained?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(88-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
11^{88}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(88-1)\bmod 6+1=4
\]

\[
2^{88}\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q211. Between \(210\) and \(377\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(13\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 210 is 210.

\[
15\times 14=210
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 375 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 377.

\[
377=15\times 25+2
\]

\[
377-2=375
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{375-210}{15}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{377}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{209}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
25-13=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{261-135}{9}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[

Q233. When \(7^{41}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(6\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(41-1)\bmod 10+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
7^{41}\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(41-1)\bmod 10+1=1
\]

\[
7^{41}\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Answer: C

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-9|=9\times 6=54
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 54.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(54\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[

\]

Fast result: \(54\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q241. Between \(156\) and \(311\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 156 is 165.

\[
15\times 11=165
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 311.

\[
311=15\times 20+11
\]

\[
311-11=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{300-165}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{311}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{155}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-10=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
480=2^{5}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(480)=1512
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 1512.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1512\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{2}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(1512\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q257. Between \(100\) and \(262\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(27\)
B. \(28\)
C. \(26\)
D. \(29\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 100 is 102.

\[
6\times 17=102
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 258 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 262.

\[
262=6\times 43+4
\]

\[
262-4=258
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{258-102}{6}+1=26+1=27
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 27.
Hence, there are 27 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{262}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{99}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
43-16=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q264. When \(2^{34}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(34-1)\bmod 10+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
2^{34}\equiv 5\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(34-1)\bmod 10+1=4
\]

\[
2^{34}\equiv 5\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(89-1)\bmod 2+1=1
\]

\[

Q290. Between \(166\) and \(263\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(15\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 166 is 168.

\[
6\times 28=168
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 258 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 263.

\[
263=6\times 43+5
\]

\[
263-5=258
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{258-168}{6}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{263}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{165}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
43-27=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

180=2^{2}\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{2}\to 2^{2}
\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{2}
\]

\[
5^{1}\to 5^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=27
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=5
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q310. After prime factorising \(18\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(6415\)
B. \(6998\)
C. \(5249\)
D. \(5832\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
18=2\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(18)=(1+1)\times (2+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 18 d=18^{d(18)/2}=18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 5832.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5832\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
18^{6/2}=5832
\]

Fast result: \(5832\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q311. Power \(11^{97}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(97-1)\bmod 6+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
11^{97}\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(97-1)\bmod 6+1=1
\]

\[
2^{97}\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[
104=2^{3}\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(56,104)=728
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(728\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(56,104)=\frac{56\times 104}{\operatorname{HCF}(56,104)}=728
\]

Fast result: \(728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q323. Between \(192\) and \(278\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 192 is 192.

\[
8\times 24=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 272 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 278.

\[
278=8\times 34+6
\]

\[
278-6=272
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{272-192}{8}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{278}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{191}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
34-23=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Situation: remainder-constrained-digit
Topology depth: 4

\]

Final Answer

\[

Fast result: \(21\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q339. Power \(3^{51}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 5.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(51-1)\bmod 5+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{51}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(51-1)\bmod 5+1=1
\]

\[
3^{51}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q347. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(11085466\)
B. \(12597120\)
C. \(15116544\)
D. \(10077696\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(36)=(2+1)\times (2+1)=9
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 36 d=36^{d(36)/2}=36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 10077696.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10077696\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Fast result: \(10077696\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

8\rightarrow 64\rightarrow 512\rightarrow 96\rightarrow 768\rightarrow 144\rightarrow 152\rightarrow 216\rightarrow 728\rightarrow 824\rightarrow 592\rightarrow 736\rightarrow 888\rightarrow 104\rightarrow 832\rightarrow 656\rightarrow 248\rightarrow 984\rightarrow 872\rightarrow 976\rightarrow 808\rightarrow 464\rightarrow 712\rightarrow 696
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(71-1)\bmod 24+1=23
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 712.

\[
8^{71}\equiv 712\pmod{1000}
\]

Answer Verification
The 23th term of the cycle is 712.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(192\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(71-1)\bmod 24+1=23
\]

\[
8^{71}\equiv 712\pmod{1000}
\]

Fast result: \(192\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q365. For the number \(30\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(1012500\)
B. \(1215000\)
C. \(810000\)
D. \(891000\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(30)=(1+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 30 d=30^{d(30)/2}=30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 810000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(810000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Fast result: \(810000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\]

Fast result: \(160\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q374. Between \(209\) and \(381\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(11\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 209 is 210.

\[
15\times 14=210
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 375 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 381.

\[
381=15\times 25+6
\]

\[
381-6=375
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{375-210}{15}+1=11+1=12
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 12.
Hence, there are 12 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{381}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{208}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
25-13=12
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[

\]

Final Answer

\[

\]

\[
3^{27}\equiv 9\pmod{11}

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q389. Between \(163\) and \(281\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(14\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 163 is 171.

\[
9\times 19=171
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 279 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 281.

\[
281=9\times 31+2
\]

\[
281-2=279
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{279-171}{9}+1=12+1=13
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 13.
Hence, there are 13 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(13\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{281}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{162}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
31-18=13
\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

C. \(81\)
D. \(79\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
40=2^{3}\times 5
\]

\[
80=2^{4}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,80)=80
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 80.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(80\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,80)=\frac{40\times 80}{\operatorname{HCF}(40,80)}=80
\]

Fast result: \(80\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

Final Answer

\[

\]

\[

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q413. Between \(178\) and \(318\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(16\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 178 is 180.

\[
9\times 20=180
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 315 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 318.

\[
318=9\times 35+3
\]

\[
318-3=315
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{315-180}{9}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{318}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{177}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
35-19=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_cyclic_pattern
Situation: range-multiple-count
Topology depth: 3
Exam mode: banking

Q441. Between \(212\) and \(364\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 212 is 225.

\[
15\times 15=225
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 364.

\[
364=15\times 24+4
\]

\[
364-4=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{360-225}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{364}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{211}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
24-14=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_cyclic_pattern
Situation: lcm-divisibility-bridge
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: elite

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-1|=9\times 6=54
\]

The required difference is 54.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(54\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

Q463. Between \(215\) and \(316\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 215 is 216.

\[
6\times 36=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 312 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 316.

\[
316=6\times 52+4
\]

\[
316-4=312
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{312-216}{6}+1=16+1=17
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 17.
Hence, there are 17 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(17\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{316}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{214}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
52-35=17
\]

Fast result: \(17\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_divisibility_multi_condition
Situation: missing-digit-rule
Topology depth: 2
Exam mode: punjab_state

Q464. Bells ring at intervals of \(66\) and \(60\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(726\)
B. \(627\)
C. \(660\)
D. \(693\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
66=2\times 3\times 11
\]

\[
60=2^{2}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(66,60)=660
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 660.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(660\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(66,60)=\frac{66\times 60}{\operatorname{HCF}(66,60)}=660
\]

Fast result: \(660\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q465. When \(3^{45}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 5.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(45-1)\bmod 5+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{45}\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(45-1)\bmod 5+1=5
\]

\[
3^{45}\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Situation: range-remainder-count
Topology depth: 4
Exam mode: ssc

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,120)=120
\]

The LCM is 120.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(120\).

Shortcut

\]

Fast result: \(120\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).
